### AutoRule: Reasoning Chain-of-thought Extracted Rule-based Rewards Improve Preference Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15651v1)
- **Authors**: Tevin Wang, Chenyan Xiong
- **Abstract**: Rule-based rewards offer a promising strategy for improving reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF), but current approaches often rely on manual rule engineering. We present AutoRule, a fully automated method for extracting rules from preference feedback and formulating them into rule-based rewards. AutoRule extraction operates in three stages: it leverages a reasoning model to interpret user preferences, identifies candidate rules from the reasoning chain of these interpretations, and synthesizes them into a unified rule set. Leveraging the finalized rule set, we employ language-model verifiers to compute the fraction of rules satisfied by each output, using this metric as an auxiliary reward alongside the learned reward model during policy optimization. Training a Llama-3-8B model with AutoRule results in a 28.6\% relative improvement in length-controlled win rate on AlpacaEval2.0, and a 6.1\% relative gain in second-turn performance on a held-out MT-Bench subset, compared to a GRPO baseline trained with the same learned reward model but without the rule-based auxiliary reward. Our analysis confirms that the extracted rules exhibit good agreement with dataset preference. We find that AutoRule demonstrates reduced reward hacking compared to a learned reward model when run over two episodes. Finally, our case study suggests that the extracted rules capture unique qualities valued in different datasets. The extracted rules are provided in the appendix, and the code is open-sourced at https://github.com/cxcscmu/AutoRule.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "AUTORULE: Reasoning Chain-of-thought Extracted Rule-based Rewards Improve Preference Learning":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AUTORULE, a novel framework for automatically extracting rules from preference feedback data to improve reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF). AUTORULE leverages the reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs) to identify rules from model-generated reasoning chains. The framework operates in three stages: reasoning generation (using an LLM to justify preferences), rule extraction (identifying rule-like statements from the reasoning chains), and rule merging (consolidating the extracted rules into a unified set). These extracted rules are then used to create a rule-based reward signal that is combined with a learned reward model during policy optimization. The paper demonstrates that training an LLama-3-8B model with AUTORULE results in improvements in length-controlled win rates on AlpacaEval2.0 and second-turn performance on a held-out MT-Bench subset compared to a GRPO baseline trained with the same learned reward model, but without the rule-based reward. The paper also presents evidence that AUTORULE reduces reward hacking and generates interpretable, dataset-adaptive rules.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the automated extraction of rules from LLM-generated reasoning chains for use as auxiliary rewards in RLHF. While rule-based rewards in RLHF are not entirely new, AUTORULE eliminates the need for manual rule engineering, which is a significant advancement. The approach of deriving rules directly from LLM reasoning chains is also a novel aspect, as most existing methods rely on handcrafted rules or large-scale crowd annotations.
*   **Significance:** The significance is several-fold. First, it reduces the reliance on expensive and time-consuming manual rule engineering. Second, the extracted rules are interpretable and adaptable to different datasets, which is crucial for aligning LLMs with diverse human preferences. Third, the paper presents empirical evidence suggesting that AUTORULE mitigates reward hacking, a well-known problem in RLHF. Furthermore, the performance gains on AlpacaEval 2.0 and MT-Bench indicates that the framework effectively improves the quality of LLM responses and enhances their ability to follow instructions.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Automated Rule Extraction:** The key strength is the automated process of extracting rules, which makes the framework scalable and cost-effective.
    *   **Interpretability:** The extracted rules are interpretable, providing insights into the underlying preferences that are being aligned with.
    *   **Adaptability:** The framework appears to be adaptable to different datasets, as demonstrated by the experiments on UltraFeedback and MT-Bench.
    *   **Reward Hacking Mitigation:** Evidence suggests that AUTORULE reduces reward hacking compared to learned reward models, enhancing the robustness of the training process.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper presents comprehensive experiments and analysis to validate the effectiveness of AUTORULE.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on LLM Reasoning Quality:** The quality of the extracted rules depends heavily on the quality of the reasoning chains generated by the LLM. If the reasoning is flawed or biased, the extracted rules may also be suboptimal.
    *   **LLM-as-a-Judge limitations:** The LLM-as-a-judge verifier, while simplifying reward modeling, may still be subject to biases or inconsistencies in its judgments, which could affect the reliability of the rule-based reward signal.
    *   **Generalizability:** While the paper shows improvements, it's important to acknowledge the challenges in generalizing across completely different tasks and modalities.
    *   **Computational Cost:** Although eliminating manual work, the process has its own computational expenses as it needs high resources from LLMs to perform the rule generation.
*   **Potential Influence:** AUTORULE has the potential to influence the field of RLHF by providing a more scalable, interpretable, and robust approach to preference alignment. The framework could be further extended to incorporate different types of feedback data, such as implicit feedback or user interactions, and to extract rules for other aspects of LLM behavior, such as safety and ethical considerations.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of RLHF. The automation of rule extraction and the evidence of reward hacking mitigation are particularly valuable. While there are limitations related to the dependency on LLM reasoning quality, the benefits of AUTORULE in terms of scalability, interpretability, and adaptability outweigh the drawbacks. The comprehensive experimental validation also supports the effectiveness of the framework.

- **Score**: 8/10

### PhishDebate: An LLM-Based Multi-Agent Framework for Phishing Website Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15656v1)
- **Authors**: Wenhao Li, Selvakumar Manickam, Yung-wey Chong, Shankar Karuppayah
- **Abstract**: Phishing websites continue to pose a significant cybersecurity threat, often leveraging deceptive structures, brand impersonation, and social engineering tactics to evade detection. While recent advances in large language models (LLMs) have enabled improved phishing detection through contextual understanding, most existing approaches rely on single-agent classification facing the risks of hallucination and lack interpretability or robustness. To address these limitations, we propose PhishDebate, a modular multi-agent LLM-based debate framework for phishing website detection. PhishDebate employs four specialized agents to independently analyze different textual aspects of a webpage--URL structure, HTML composition, semantic content, and brand impersonation--under the coordination of a Moderator and a final Judge. Through structured debate and divergent thinking, the framework delivers more accurate and interpretable decisions. Extensive evaluations on commercial LLMs demonstrate that PhishDebate achieves 98.2% recall and 98.2% True Positive Rate (TPR) on a real-world phishing dataset, and outperforms single-agent and Chain of Thought (CoT) baselines. Additionally, its modular design allows agent-level configurability, enabling adaptation to varying resource and application requirements.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "PhishDebate: An LLM-Based Multi-Agent Framework for Phishing Website Detection":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PhishDebate, a novel multi-agent LLM-based framework designed to improve the accuracy and interpretability of phishing website detection. The framework employs four specialized agents (URL Analyst, HTML Structure, Content Semantic, and Brand Impersonation) that independently analyze different aspects of a website. A Moderator coordinates the debate process, and a Judge renders the final classification decision. The paper demonstrates through experiments with commercial LLMs that PhishDebate achieves high recall and accuracy, outperforming single-agent and Chain-of-Thought (CoT) baselines. The modular design also allows for agent-level configurability to adapt to various resource and application needs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core idea of using a multi-agent debate framework for phishing website detection is relatively novel. Existing LLM-based phishing detection mostly uses single-agent approaches.  The idea of structured debate and collaborative reasoning inspired by human deliberation is a good one.
*   **Modularity and Flexibility:** PhishDebate's modular design is a significant strength. The ability to exclude or include specific agents based on resource constraints or task requirements makes the framework adaptable to diverse environments.  This also facilitates ablation studies to assess the contribution of each agent, which is demonstrated in the paper.
*   **Improved Performance:** The experimental results show that PhishDebate consistently outperforms single-agent and CoT baselines. This highlights the effectiveness of the debate-driven approach in enhancing phishing detection accuracy and robustness. The high recall (98.2%) is particularly valuable as it minimizes the risk of false negatives, which can have severe consequences in phishing attacks.
*   **Interpretability:** One of the key advantages of PhishDebate is its enhanced interpretability. The structured debate process provides detailed reasoning chains from each agent, allowing analysts to understand the factors that contributed to the final decision. The case study shows how decisions are made and rationalized in the debate.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper provides a thorough evaluation of the framework using two real-world phishing datasets and four commercial LLMs. The comparative analysis with baselines and the scenario analysis further strengthen the validity of the results.
*   **Addresses limitations of LLMs**: By encouraging divergent thinking among specialized agents, PhishDebate helps to mitigate risks of hallucination and bias that are common in single-agent LLM systems.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Complexity:** The multi-agent approach inevitably increases the system's complexity compared to simpler single-agent solutions. Managing and coordinating the interactions between agents can be challenging.
*   **Dependency on LLM Capabilities:** PhishDebate relies heavily on the reasoning and analytical capabilities of the underlying LLMs. The performance of the framework is directly affected by the quality and reliability of these models.
*   **Computational Cost:** The debate process requires multiple LLM queries, which can be computationally expensive and time-consuming, especially with larger models. While the configurable debate rounds and early termination mechanisms help mitigate this, computational cost remains a consideration. The table indicates the processing time by each model, which is much longer than the single-agent approaches.
*   **Limited Scope of Scenario Analysis:** While the scenario analysis provides insights into the importance of different agents, the scope could be expanded to explore the impact of various debate strategies, agent prompting techniques, and consensus evaluation methods.
*   **Lack of Focus on Adversarial Scenarios**: While the paper mentions future research will explore the system's resilience against adversarial phishing samples, the current evaluation does not address the framework's performance under adversarial attacks or evasion techniques.

**Significance:**

PhishDebate presents a significant step forward in LLM-based phishing website detection. By addressing the limitations of single-agent approaches and incorporating the benefits of collaborative reasoning, the framework enhances accuracy, interpretability, and adaptability. The modular design and comprehensive evaluation further contribute to the practical value of the research. The work has the potential to influence the development of more robust and transparent phishing detection systems.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:**

PhishDebate is a well-executed and valuable contribution to the field. It demonstrates a clear improvement over existing approaches and offers a novel framework that could be beneficial in real-world phishing detection scenarios. The evaluation is rigorous and supports the claims made in the paper. However, the framework's complexity and reliance on LLM resources are limitations that need to be considered. While the novelty is substantial, there are still avenues to explore especially in the domain of adversarial robustness, prompting techniques, and efficiency improvements. Therefore, a score of 7.5 reflects the significance of the work while acknowledging the need for future research to address remaining challenges.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### CC-LEARN: Cohort-based Consistency Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15662v1)
- **Authors**: Xiao Ye, Shaswat Shrivastava, Zhaonan Li, Jacob Dineen, Shijie Lu, Avneet Ahuja, Ming Shen, Zhikun Xu, Ben Zhou
- **Abstract**: Large language models excel at many tasks but still struggle with consistent, robust reasoning. We introduce Cohort-based Consistency Learning (CC-Learn), a reinforcement learning framework that improves the reliability of LLM reasoning by training on cohorts of similar questions derived from shared programmatic abstractions. To enforce cohort-level consistency, we define a composite objective combining cohort accuracy, a retrieval bonus for effective problem decomposition, and a rejection penalty for trivial or invalid lookups that reinforcement learning can directly optimize, unlike supervised fine-tuning. Optimizing this reward guides the model to adopt uniform reasoning patterns across all cohort members. Experiments on challenging reasoning benchmarks (including ARC-Challenge and StrategyQA) show that CC-Learn boosts both accuracy and reasoning stability over pretrained and SFT baselines. These results demonstrate that cohort-level RL effectively enhances reasoning consistency in LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here is a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper "CC-LEARN: Cohort-based Consistency Learning":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CC-LEARN, a reinforcement learning framework designed to improve the consistency and robustness of reasoning in Large Language Models (LLMs).  CC-LEARN operates by training LLMs on "cohorts" of similar questions derived from shared programmatic abstractions. The framework employs a composite reward function that optimizes for cohort-level accuracy, encourages effective problem decomposition through a retrieval bonus, and penalizes trivial or invalid lookups.  This approach aims to guide the model towards adopting uniform and verifiable reasoning patterns across all cohort members, thereby enhancing reasoning consistency. Experiments on various reasoning benchmarks demonstrate that CC-LEARN improves both accuracy and reasoning stability compared to pre-trained and supervised fine-tuned baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using cohorts of similar questions to enforce consistency is a valuable contribution. The approach tackles a real weakness of current LLMs and moves beyond the standard self-consistency approaches, which only address consistency *within* a single generation and not across different formulations of the same problem. The use of programmatic abstractions to generate these cohorts is also a novel and effective strategy for controlling the types of reasoning the model must learn.

*   **Significance:**  Inconsistent reasoning is a major impediment to the deployment of LLMs in many real-world applications. This work offers a concrete way to mitigate this, potentially leading to more reliable and trustworthy LLM-based systems. By focusing on verifiable reasoning procedures instead of surface-level pattern matching, CC-LEARN addresses a fundamental problem in LLM research. The results demonstrating significant gains on challenging reasoning benchmarks suggest the approach has a practical impact.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined framework with a clear objective function.
    *   Effective use of reinforcement learning to optimize for consistency.
    *   Strong empirical results across a diverse set of challenging benchmarks.
    *   Ablation studies that highlight the importance of similar-question training.
    * Human evaluation demonstrating the improved quality of reasoning paths.
    *The architectural separation between the policy and retriever model ensures reasoning strategies are not directly influenced by factual information.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Complexity: The framework involves multiple components (abstraction generation, cohort creation, program synthesis, reinforcement learning), which may make it difficult to implement and scale.
    *   Computational cost: RL training can be resource-intensive. The paper touches on this but does not provide detailed analysis of the computational footprint.
    *   Reliance on retrieval: The framework relies on a retrieval component, which could introduce noise and limit the scope of reasoning. While the rejection prompts help with controlling this, it is still a core reliance.
    * The limitation section admits that only one hyperparameter setup was employed and the hyperparameter search could have been exhaustive.

*   **Potential Impact:**  The approach has the potential to significantly improve the reliability and trustworthiness of LLMs, making them more suitable for a broader range of applications, especially those requiring high accuracy and consistency. The method also offers a pathway towards more interpretable and controllable LLM reasoning.

*   **Rigorous Rationale for score:** The paper presents a solid approach for improving the consistency of LLMs' reasoning using an RL-based cohort learning framework. The methodology appears well-designed, and the results demonstrate substantial improvements over existing methods across multiple benchmarks. Despite the potential limitations related to complexity and computational cost, the significance of the consistency problem and the effectiveness of the proposed solution warrant a high rating.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### SwarmAgentic: Towards Fully Automated Agentic System Generation via Swarm Intelligence
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15672v1)
- **Authors**: Yao Zhang, Chenyang Lin, Shijie Tang, Haokun Chen, Shijie Zhou, Yunpu Ma, Volker Tresp
- **Abstract**: The rapid progress of Large Language Models has advanced agentic systems in decision-making, coordination, and task execution. Yet, existing agentic system generation frameworks lack full autonomy, missing from-scratch agent generation, self-optimizing agent functionality, and collaboration, limiting adaptability and scalability. We propose SwarmAgentic, a framework for fully automated agentic system generation that constructs agentic systems from scratch and jointly optimizes agent functionality and collaboration as interdependent components through language-driven exploration. To enable efficient search over system-level structures, SwarmAgentic maintains a population of candidate systems and evolves them via feedback-guided updates, drawing inspiration from Particle Swarm Optimization (PSO). We evaluate our method on six real-world, open-ended, and exploratory tasks involving high-level planning, system-level coordination, and creative reasoning. Given only a task description and an objective function, SwarmAgentic outperforms all baselines, achieving a +261.8% relative improvement over ADAS on the TravelPlanner benchmark, highlighting the effectiveness of full automation in structurally unconstrained tasks. This framework marks a significant step toward scalable and autonomous agentic system design, bridging swarm intelligence with fully automated system multi-agent generation. Our code is publicly released at https://yaoz720.github.io/SwarmAgentic/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SwarmAgentic, a novel framework for fully automated agentic system generation. Unlike existing approaches that rely on predefined templates, seed agents, or human intervention, SwarmAgentic constructs agentic systems from scratch and optimizes both agent functionality and collaboration using a language-driven adaptation of Particle Swarm Optimization (PSO). It uses LLMs to guide system-level structure exploration. Specifically, it maintains a population of candidate systems (particles) and evolves them using feedback-guided updates based on performance evaluations, failure identification, and mechanisms for learning from past successes and swarm knowledge. The paper presents experimental results on six diverse, open-ended tasks (Travel Planning, Meeting Planning, Creative Writing, etc.), demonstrating SwarmAgentic's superior performance compared to existing prompting and automated agent generation methods. The code is publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core novelty of SwarmAgentic lies in its complete automation of agentic system generation, fulfilling three key criteria:  from-scratch agent generation, self-optimizing agent functionality, and self-optimizing agent collaboration.  Existing frameworks typically address only one or two of these.  The adaptation of PSO to a language-based design space is also a significant contribution, allowing for structured exploration of complex system configurations.
*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from the potential to drastically reduce engineering overhead and enable scalable and autonomous agentic system design. By eliminating manual intervention and predefined structures, SwarmAgentic allows for the emergence of self-optimizing system behaviors and greater adaptability to diverse and complex task specifications. The open-ended experimental tasks demonstrate the framework's practical applicability to real-world scenarios.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Full Automation:** The paper successfully demonstrates a framework that requires only a task description and objective function as input, removing reliance on human expertise or pre-designed components.
    *   **Language-Driven PSO:** The innovative use of language-based transformations within the PSO framework allows for interpretable optimization and exploration of complex, non-differentiable design spaces.
    *   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper presents convincing empirical evidence demonstrating SwarmAgentic's superior performance across a diverse set of tasks. The +261.8% relative improvement over ADAS on the TravelPlanner benchmark is impressive.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the importance of key components, such as failure-driven adjustments, agent-level adaptation, and collaborative structures reconfiguration.
    *   **Cross-Model Transferability:**  The analysis of cross-model transferability further strengthens the generalizability of the method.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on LLMs:** SwarmAgentic's performance is heavily reliant on the capabilities of the underlying LLMs.  The authors acknowledge the limitations of LLMs, particularly in factual reliability and grounded interaction.  Further research is needed to address these inherent weaknesses and explore potential integration with external knowledge sources or embodied agents. The reliance on GPT-4 might create an availability concern and should explore other models or architectures.
    *   **Lack of Formal Guarantees:** The paper lacks formal guarantees on the optimality or convergence of the language-driven PSO process. While empirical results are strong, a theoretical analysis of the optimization properties would enhance the credibility of the approach.
    *   **Limited Evaluation of Scalability:**  While the paper claims scalability as a key benefit, the experiments do not explicitly evaluate the framework's performance as the number of agents or the complexity of tasks increases dramatically.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper presents a significant and novel contribution to the field of agentic systems. The complete automation of agentic system generation, coupled with the language-driven PSO approach, has the potential to transform how these systems are designed and deployed. The experiments are strong, and the ablation studies provide valuable insights. The main weakness is the reliance on the capabilities of LLMs and the lack of formal guarantees for the optimization process. While these are important considerations, they do not detract from the overall impact of the work.

### UniRelight: Learning Joint Decomposition and Synthesis for Video Relighting
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15673v1)
- **Authors**: Kai He, Ruofan Liang, Jacob Munkberg, Jon Hasselgren, Nandita Vijaykumar, Alexander Keller, Sanja Fidler, Igor Gilitschenski, Zan Gojcic, Zian Wang
- **Abstract**: We address the challenge of relighting a single image or video, a task that demands precise scene intrinsic understanding and high-quality light transport synthesis. Existing end-to-end relighting models are often limited by the scarcity of paired multi-illumination data, restricting their ability to generalize across diverse scenes. Conversely, two-stage pipelines that combine inverse and forward rendering can mitigate data requirements but are susceptible to error accumulation and often fail to produce realistic outputs under complex lighting conditions or with sophisticated materials. In this work, we introduce a general-purpose approach that jointly estimates albedo and synthesizes relit outputs in a single pass, harnessing the generative capabilities of video diffusion models. This joint formulation enhances implicit scene comprehension and facilitates the creation of realistic lighting effects and intricate material interactions, such as shadows, reflections, and transparency. Trained on synthetic multi-illumination data and extensive automatically labeled real-world videos, our model demonstrates strong generalization across diverse domains and surpasses previous methods in both visual fidelity and temporal consistency.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the UniRelight paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces UniRelight, a novel framework for video relighting that jointly estimates scene albedo and synthesizes relit outputs.  Instead of using a two-stage inverse/forward rendering pipeline common in the field, UniRelight utilizes a single-pass approach based on a video diffusion model. This joint formulation aims to improve implicit scene understanding and facilitate the creation of realistic lighting effects including shadows, reflections, and transparency. The model is trained using a combination of synthetic multi-illumination data and automatically labeled real-world videos, allowing for generalization across diverse domains. The results show the method performs well in terms of visual fidelity and temporal consistency, surpassing previous methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the *joint* estimation of albedo and relit video within a single pass of a video diffusion model, replacing the traditional two-stage pipelines.  While other recent works have incorporated diffusion models into relighting (e.g., DiffusionRenderer), UniRelight appears to be unique in its explicit joint modeling strategy and its focus on leveraging readily available auto-labeled real-world video. Using VideoJAM's approach on denosing latents for albedo and relighting in a single pass is also novel.
*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from its ability to address the limitations of previous methods. By training models on both synthetic and automatically labelled real-world videos the model manages to overcome the limitation of the requirement of multi-illumination datasets. The approach demonstrates state-of-the-art performance and provides more accurate and visually compelling results.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Joint Modeling:**  The joint modeling approach appears to be a key strength, as the ablation studies highlight that is significantly improves the results and ensures that the shadows and specular highlights are not baked in from the original image. The ablation studies confirm that jointly modelling a relit image with an albedo image helps to improve results.
    *   **Data Strategy:** The data curation strategy using a mix of synthetic data (for supervision and covering the lighting space) and auto-labeled real-world data to help achieve better realness is a critical aspect of the paper.
    *   **Results:** The qualitative and quantitative results are compelling, demonstrating the model's ability to generalize to different scenes and outcompete existing methods.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Runtime:** The main shortcoming appears to be the time required.
    *   **Limitations:** The model cannot handle emittance from objects in the environment and relies on environmental lighting.

*   **Impact:** This work provides a compelling alternative to existing relighting methods, offering more realistic and visually coherent results and has good generalizability. The joint approach, use of automatically labeled data, and open architecture make this valuable for researchers.

*   **Rigor:** The experiments appear well designed and executed, with appropriate baselines, datasets, and metrics. The ablation studies are especially important in demonstrating the benefits of the proposed approach. The inclusion of a user study provides further validation of the perceptual quality.

*   **Clarity:** The paper is generally well-written and easy to understand. The approach and architecture diagrams are easy to follow, and the experimental setup is clearly explained. The use of ablations to assess different design options is effective.

**Justification for Score:**

Given its novelty in joint modeling, the significance of providing a realistic approach to video relighting, and the rigor of the experiments, this paper represents a significant contribution. The model also overcomes the limitations of previous works in training for real-world images by training the model using automatically labelled data. While there are some limitations, the strengths are very compelling.

### GenRecal: Generation after Recalibration from Large to Small Vision-Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15681v1)
- **Authors**: Byung-Kwan Lee, Ryo Hachiuma, Yong Man Ro, Yu-Chiang Frank Wang, Yueh-Hua Wu
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in vision-language models (VLMs) have leveraged large language models (LLMs) to achieve performance on par with closed-source systems like GPT-4V. However, deploying these models in real-world scenarios, particularly on resource-constrained devices, remains challenging due to their substantial computational demands. This has spurred interest in distilling knowledge from large VLMs into smaller, more efficient counterparts. A key challenge arises here from the diversity of VLM architectures, which are built on different LLMs and employ varying token types-differing in vocabulary size, token splits, and token index ordering. To address this challenge of limitation to a specific VLM type, we present Generation after Recalibration (GenRecal), a novel, general-purpose distillation framework for VLMs. GenRecal incorporates a Recalibrator that aligns and adapts feature representations between heterogeneous VLMs, enabling effective knowledge transfer across different types of VLMs. Through extensive experiments on multiple challenging benchmarks, we demonstrate that GenRecal significantly improves baseline performances, eventually outperforming large-scale open- and closed-source VLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the GenRecal paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GenRecal, a novel distillation framework for Vision-Language Models (VLMs) designed to overcome limitations in existing methods.  A key challenge in VLM distillation is architectural heterogeneity – different VLMs use different language models (LLMs), tokenizers (vocabulary sizes, token splits, ordering), and image embedding strategies. GenRecal addresses this by introducing a "Recalibrator" module that aligns and adapts feature representations between large (teacher) and small (student) VLMs, regardless of their underlying architectures. The paper presents experiments across various challenging benchmarks showing that GenRecal outperforms traditional distillation methods, and even surpasses larger open-source and closed-source VLMs. The core claim is that this feature alignment enables effective knowledge transfer even when VLMs have different token types.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**
    * **Addresses a Practical Problem:** The paper tackles a real-world bottleneck in VLM deployment: the computational cost of large models and the difficulty in distilling knowledge across heterogeneous architectures.  The growing diversity of VLM designs makes a generalized distillation technique highly desirable.
    * **Novel Approach:** The "Recalibrator" is a sensible innovation. By focusing on aligning feature representations *before* distillation, the method neatly sidesteps the complexities arising from differing tokenization schemes. The results seemingly support the effectiveness of this approach.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experiments cover a good range of benchmarks (MMB, MM-Vet, MMMU, etc.) and VLM architectures (Qwen2, InternVL, NVLM) with varying sizes. This provides empirical evidence for the general applicability of GenRecal.  Ablation studies analyzing the importance of the regularization term in the Recalibrator are also beneficial.
    * **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-structured and provides enough implementation detail for reproducibility.  The figures and tables clearly illustrate the key ideas and results.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Incremental Novelty (Potentially):** While the "Recalibrator" is presented as novel, feature alignment in transfer learning and distillation is a well-explored area. The paper needs to more thoroughly differentiate GenRecal from prior approaches. Specifically, more analysis is needed to explain *why* the proposed Recalibrator is better than simply using a fully connected layer or other standard adaptation techniques.
    * **Limited Theoretical Justification:** The paper relies heavily on empirical results. A more rigorous theoretical justification for the design of the Recalibrator and its alignment properties would significantly strengthen the contribution.  Why these specific layers, this architecture, and this training process?
    * **Ablation Study Depth:** Although the authors include ablation studies, the impact of architectural choices within the "Recalibrator" are somewhat lacking. It would be beneficial to see if the decoder layers' dimensions are critical or if an MLP performs equivalently. More ablations can help understand the Recalibrator's sensitivity to hyperparameter tuning.
    * **Missing Baselines/Comparisons:** While compared to other distillation methods, direct comparison to knowledge transfer/domain adaptation literature using similar techniques to Recalibrator would be beneficial.

* **Significance:**
    * If the Recalibrator truly delivers on its promise of generalized knowledge transfer, the impact could be considerable. It would simplify the development of smaller, task-specific VLMs by allowing researchers and practitioners to leverage the best available large models, irrespective of their architectural details.
    * It could lead to improved on-device AI capabilities, making multimodal AI accessible in resource-constrained settings.
    * The open-source nature of the implementation (mentioned in the paper) will facilitate wider adoption and further research.

**Justification of Score:**

While the paper addresses an important problem and presents an interesting solution, the lack of strong theoretical justification and the somewhat incremental novelty compared to existing feature alignment techniques prevents a very high score.  It would be improved by detailed comparisons with the knowledge transfer methods.  Even with thorough experimentation, the work requires additional analysis to fully appreciate the merits and limitations of GenRecal.

Score: 6.5

- **Score**: 6/10

### Evolutionary Caching to Accelerate Your Off-the-Shelf Diffusion Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15682v1)
- **Authors**: Anirud Aggarwal, Abhinav Shrivastava, Matthew Gwilliam
- **Abstract**: Diffusion-based image generation models excel at producing high-quality synthetic content, but suffer from slow and computationally expensive inference. Prior work has attempted to mitigate this by caching and reusing features within diffusion transformers across inference steps. These methods, however, often rely on rigid heuristics that result in limited acceleration or poor generalization across architectures. We propose Evolutionary Caching to Accelerate Diffusion models (ECAD), a genetic algorithm that learns efficient, per-model, caching schedules forming a Pareto frontier, using only a small set of calibration prompts. ECAD requires no modifications to network parameters or reference images. It offers significant inference speedups, enables fine-grained control over the quality-latency trade-off, and adapts seamlessly to different diffusion models. Notably, ECAD's learned schedules can generalize effectively to resolutions and model variants not seen during calibration. We evaluate ECAD on PixArt-alpha, PixArt-Sigma, and FLUX-1.dev using multiple metrics (FID, CLIP, Image Reward) across diverse benchmarks (COCO, MJHQ-30k, PartiPrompts), demonstrating consistent improvements over previous approaches. On PixArt-alpha, ECAD identifies a schedule that outperforms the previous state-of-the-art method by 4.47 COCO FID while increasing inference speedup from 2.35x to 2.58x. Our results establish ECAD as a scalable and generalizable approach for accelerating diffusion inference. Our project website is available at https://aniaggarwal.github.io/ecad and our code is available at https://github.com/aniaggarwal/ecad.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Evolutionary Caching to Accelerate Diffusion models (ECAD), a genetic algorithm-based approach for learning efficient caching schedules in diffusion transformers. Unlike existing heuristic-based methods, ECAD optimizes for a Pareto frontier of image quality vs. inference speed, requiring only a small set of calibration prompts and no modifications to the network parameters or reference images. The authors demonstrate that ECAD can significantly improve inference speedups, enable fine-grained control over the quality-latency trade-off, and adapt seamlessly to different diffusion models, generalizing effectively to resolutions and model variants not seen during calibration.  The approach is evaluated on PixArt-α, PixArt-Σ, and FLUX-1.dev using metrics like FID, CLIP, and Image Reward.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in reframing the diffusion caching problem as a multi-objective optimization task and using a genetic algorithm to find Pareto-optimal caching schedules. While caching in diffusion models isn't new, automating the discovery of caching strategies in a principled, optimization-driven way is a significant departure from prior heuristic approaches. The idea of an evolutionary strategy is interesting and offers a higher degree of automation than manually-tuned approaches. This offers a substantial advantage in generalization to new models.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial challenge in diffusion models: their high computational cost. Faster inference is a key enabler for broader adoption and real-world applications.  The ability of ECAD to adapt to different architectures and resolutions without retraining or manual tuning is significant. The claimed improvement in speed/quality trade-offs is important for practical usage. The generality of the method and the lack of modification of the underlying model are also important. However, the method relies heavily on the quality of the ImageReward metric, and its influence requires more in-depth analysis.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Principled approach:**  Replaces heuristics with a systematic optimization framework.
    *   **Generalizability:** Demonstrates strong performance across different models and resolutions.
    *   **Flexibility:** Allows for fine-grained control over the quality-latency trade-off.
    *   **Automation:**  Minimizes manual tuning and model-specific engineering.
    *   The approach can be applied asynchronously.
    *   Requires low memory and can be applied on small GPUs.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependence on Image Reward:** The algorithm's effectiveness relies heavily on the quality and accuracy of the Image Reward metric.  The authors need to provide a more in-depth analysis of the impact of different quality metrics on the final caching schedules.
    *   **Computational Cost of Calibration:** The evolutionary process, while automated, requires a significant number of image generations during the calibration phase. While it can run without gradient computation, it may still be difficult to run for some users.
    *   **Limited Architectural Diversity:** Experiments cover relatively similar DiT variants.  Performance on radically different diffusion architectures (e.g., those with U-Nets) should be investigated.
    *   While the proposed solution outperforms other solutions in the same solution class (training-free methods for diffusion acceleration), it does not explicitly compare the result with training based method. A discussion on this class of method is needed.

*   **Potential Influence:**  ECAD has the potential to become a standard tool for accelerating diffusion inference, enabling faster and more efficient generation of high-quality images. Its ability to adapt to new architectures and resolutions could make it a valuable asset for researchers and practitioners working with diffusion models.

* **Justification:** While the paper provides a solid contribution by automating the caching schedule for diffusion models, it relies heavily on the quality of the ImageReward metric, and it is not fully investigated and analysed. Additionally, the improvement that is possible with a trainable module is not investigated in detail. These limitations in the analysis warrant a slightly lower score than initially considered.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### PhantomHunter: Detecting Unseen Privately-Tuned LLM-Generated Text via Family-Aware Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15683v1)
- **Authors**: Yuhui Shi, Yehan Yang, Qiang Sheng, Hao Mi, Beizhe Hu, Chaoxi Xu, Juan Cao
- **Abstract**: With the popularity of large language models (LLMs), undesirable societal problems like misinformation production and academic misconduct have been more severe, making LLM-generated text detection now of unprecedented importance. Although existing methods have made remarkable progress, a new challenge posed by text from privately tuned LLMs remains underexplored. Users could easily possess private LLMs by fine-tuning an open-source one with private corpora, resulting in a significant performance drop of existing detectors in practice. To address this issue, we propose PhantomHunter, an LLM-generated text detector specialized for detecting text from unseen, privately-tuned LLMs. Its family-aware learning framework captures family-level traits shared across the base models and their derivatives, instead of memorizing individual characteristics. Experiments on data from LLaMA, Gemma, and Mistral families show its superiority over 7 baselines and 3 industrial services, with F1 scores of over 96%.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "PhantomHunter: Detecting Unseen Privately-Tuned LLM-Generated Text via Family-Aware Learning" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of detecting text generated by privately fine-tuned large language models (LLMs).  It argues that existing LLM-generated text (LLMGT) detectors often fail when faced with text from privately fine-tuned models because the fine-tuning process alters the characteristics learned by the detectors. To overcome this, the authors propose PhantomHunter, a detector that uses "family-aware" learning.  PhantomHunter captures family-level traits shared across base models and their fine-tuned derivatives, rather than trying to memorize individual characteristics of specific models.  The core idea is that fine-tuning, while changing certain aspects, preserves underlying characteristics related to the base model. PhantomHunter consists of three main components: a base probability feature extractor, a contrastive learning-based family encoder, and a mixture-of-experts detection module. Experimental results on data from LLaMA, Gemma, and Mistral families show that PhantomHunter outperforms several baselines and industrial services.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of "family-aware learning" is moderately novel. While previous works have considered the influence of different base LLMs on detectability, directly addressing the issue of privately fine-tuned derivatives through a dedicated architecture and training strategy is a valuable contribution. The idea of capturing family-level similarities in probability distributions through a contrastive approach to improve the detection of LLM generated text after fine-tuning, presents a compelling method.
*   **Significance:** The problem the paper tackles is highly significant. The rise of open-source LLMs and easy fine-tuning tools makes it crucial to detect text generated by privately customized models, which could be used to spread misinformation or commit academic fraud. The paper offers a practical solution to a real-world problem and can thus have an impact on the community. The empirical demonstration of existing methods failing against fine-tuned models convincingly establishes the significance of this work.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Well-defined problem:** The paper clearly identifies a critical and emerging problem in LLMGT detection.
    *   **Comprehensive experiments:** The paper conducts thorough experiments comparing PhantomHunter with various baselines, including both open-source methods and commercial services, on multiple LLM families.
    *   **Ablation studies:** The ablation studies offer insights into the contribution of each component of the proposed architecture.
    *   **Clear writing and organization:** The paper is generally well-written and logically structured.
    *   **Detailed analysis:** Section 4.5 provides valuable further analyses that strengthen the paper.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited generalization:** The evaluation focuses on three LLM families (LLaMA, Gemma, Mistral). While these are popular, the extent to which the "family-aware" approach generalizes to less-studied or more significantly modified models remains unclear. The paper acknowledges this limitation but further investigation into the generalizability would strengthen the claims.
    *   **Dependence on base model availability:** The method relies on having access to the base LLMs to extract probability features. In scenarios where the base model is not readily available or is proprietary, PhantomHunter's applicability is limited.
    *   **Limited analysis of the nature of family-level traits:** The paper empirically demonstrates the existence of these traits, but doesn't deeply analyze *what* specific probability patterns or features constitute the "family trait." Understanding this more precisely could lead to further improvements in the detection method.
    *   **No comparisons against prompt-engineering-resistant detection approaches:** Recent work explores techniques that resist detection via prompt engineering. A comparison would showcase how PhantomHunter holds up in the broader spectrum of potential attacks against LLM detectors.
    *   **Computational cost:** Extracting features from multiple base LLMs adds to the computational cost of detection. A more detailed analysis of the inference time would be beneficial.
*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper's ideas and results are likely to influence the development of more robust and general LLMGT detectors. The concept of family-aware learning could be adopted and adapted by other researchers. The paper could serve as a starting point for investigating methods that are resilient to fine-tuning and other model modifications.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper makes a tangible contribution by addressing a significant and emerging problem in the field. PhantomHunter presents a sensible and effective approach, supported by solid experimental results. However, the limitations regarding generalizability, dependence on base models, and lack of deeper analysis of family-level traits prevent it from being a truly exceptional contribution. Therefore, I believe a score of 7 is appropriate.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Nabla-R2D3: Effective and Efficient 3D Diffusion Alignment with 2D Rewards
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15684v1)
- **Authors**: Qingming Liu, Zhen Liu, Dinghuai Zhang, Kui Jia
- **Abstract**: Generating high-quality and photorealistic 3D assets remains a longstanding challenge in 3D vision and computer graphics. Although state-of-the-art generative models, such as diffusion models, have made significant progress in 3D generation, they often fall short of human-designed content due to limited ability to follow instructions, align with human preferences, or produce realistic textures, geometries, and physical attributes. In this paper, we introduce Nabla-R2D3, a highly effective and sample-efficient reinforcement learning alignment framework for 3D-native diffusion models using 2D rewards. Built upon the recently proposed Nabla-GFlowNet method, which matches the score function to reward gradients in a principled manner for reward finetuning, our Nabla-R2D3 enables effective adaptation of 3D diffusion models using only 2D reward signals. Extensive experiments show that, unlike vanilla finetuning baselines which either struggle to converge or suffer from reward hacking, Nabla-R2D3 consistently achieves higher rewards and reduced prior forgetting within a few finetuning steps.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Nabla-R2D3, a novel reinforcement learning (RL) based alignment framework for 3D-native diffusion models.  It aims to improve the quality of 3D assets generated by these models by aligning them with human preferences using 2D reward signals. Nabla-R2D3 builds on the Nabla-GFlowNet method, adapting it to fine-tune 3D diffusion models based on rewards derived from 2D renderings. The paper demonstrates the effectiveness of using various 2D reward models (appearance-based, geometry-based) to guide the fine-tuning process.  Experiments show Nabla-R2D3 achieves higher rewards, better prior preservation, and improved text-object alignment compared to vanilla fine-tuning baselines, while also reducing geometric artifacts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using 2D reward models to fine-tune 3D-native diffusion models is moderately novel.  While the "lifting from 2D" concept is not entirely new in 3D generation, applying it to *reward fine-tuning* in a principled RL framework like Nabla-GFlowNet *and* using differentiable 2D rewards represents a specific and useful combination of ideas. Using a combination of geometry-based and appearance based 2D differentiable rewards is also a valuable contribution. The connection to SDS is also a clear, well-described advantage.

*   **Significance:** Addressing the lack of high-quality 3D reward models is a significant challenge. By circumventing the need for explicit 3D reward models, Nabla-R2D3 makes 3D asset generation more practical and accessible. The demonstrated improvements in visual fidelity, prior preservation, and text alignment compared to baselines are meaningful. The reduction in geometric artifacts is also important as these artifacts are a common problem in 3D generation. The paper is impactful because it presents a practical method for aligning complex generative models with human preferences. This alignment is often crucial for the ultimate usability of any generated content.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Principled Approach:** Using Nabla-GFlowNet provides a more theoretically sound and stable method for reward fine-tuning compared to basic policy gradient methods or direct optimization.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments comprehensively evaluate the method across various reward models, base models, and metrics. The qualitative results are compelling.
    *   **Practicality:** The method is shown to be efficient, requiring fewer fine-tuning steps and exhibiting robustness against overfitting. This is an important consideration for real-world applications.
    *   **Well-written and Clear:** The paper is structured logically and presents the technical details clearly.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependency on 2D Reward Models:** While avoiding 3D reward models is a strength, the method's performance relies heavily on the quality and expressiveness of the 2D reward models. It's not clear how well the method would generalize to tasks where suitable 2D reward models are not available.
    *   **Limited Scope of Geometry Rewards:** The geometry rewards used seem limited to normal consistency and depth consistency. Exploring more advanced geometric priors or physical constraints could further enhance the method's capabilities.
    *   **Potential for Bias:** While addressing the lack of high-quality 3D data, using the 2D view lifting opens itself to biases from the 2D datasets and pretrained diffusion models used.

*   **Potential Influence:** The Nabla-R2D3 framework could become a standard technique for aligning 3D diffusion models with human preferences, particularly in scenarios where obtaining high-quality 3D training data is prohibitive. It could spur further research into developing better 2D reward models for 3D asset generation and inspire new methods for incorporating geometric priors into diffusion models.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

Nabla-R2D3 fills a clear gap in the 3D generation space by providing a practical approach to reward fine-tuning, sidestepping the challenges associated with building 3D reward models. While not entirely groundbreaking in its individual components, the way the paper combines these elements (lifted 2D rewards, GFlowNet based optimization) is effective and impactful. The empirical results, demonstrating superior performance and reduced artifacts, solidify the usefulness of the proposed framework. The weaknesses – dependence on 2D reward models and potential for 2D based biases – are important caveats but do not detract substantially from the overall value of the contribution. The potential impact on the field of 3D generation and the promise of creating more controllable and preference-aligned 3D assets make this a solid and noteworthy piece of work.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 7/10
